A rise is a signal: a frame changed on one LAN, two senders that share a MAC, or a benign per-LAN difference, such as a PTP event message whose correction field differs per LAN if a device puts an RCT on PTP (D2's N-2, inference; whether a SEL device does is unknown).
  - **`prp_table_full`:** frames passed without a duplicate check, because the table was full. A frame that cannot be checked is passed, never discarded unchecked: a duplicate then doubles a count, which shows, where an unchecked discard could lose a frame, which would not.
  - **`prp_tag_differs`:** discarded duplicates whose 802.1Q tag differed from the kept copy's (D2's condition 1). The node's status shows it beside `prp_table_full` while it is above 0, as a reason why that sensor's flow counts and VLAN sets are unreliable. That is D2's note on round two, for D5 to build.
  - **`prp_wrong_lan`:** frames whose RCT LAN ID is not the configured `lan` of the interface they arrived on, the interface being the slot that the frame's pipe record carries (SL-3). They are handled as any other frame. The SEL-751 counts such "WRONG LAN" frames too (D2 on #627).
- **The table.** `prp_table_entries` is the number of (MAC, sequence number) entries kept: 65,536 by default, from 4 to 1,048,576 (D6's bounds note).
  - An entry lives for the window only, so the table holds the doubly attached sources' frame rate times `prp_window_ms` (D2). At 400 ms, 65,536 entries hold about 163,000 frames a second.
  - D2's figures: one SV stream at 80 samples a cycle is 4,800 frames a second at 60 Hz (inference, IEC 61850-9-2LE not held), so sixteen streams fill under a third of the table. A line-rate flood of minimum-size frames can fill it, and `prp_table_full` then counts. It is a `setting` line in the manifest (CH-32), so the rig can set 4 (P-3).
- **Not in `coverage.degraded`.** `prp_table_full` can double counts while `coverage.degraded` is empty (D6). A new degraded value would make every 0.1 reader refuse the stored 0.2 records that carry it, because the reader checks the degraded list against its own closed set at every minor (`citation.go`). So the counter, the node's status and its `sensor.counters` event carry it instead (§16).
- **Meets:** SL-3. **Test:** P-2, P-3.

**CH-30. More than one sensor host on a PRP network: what C13 would need, as options** (D2's N-3).

D3's O-12 lists one host with two monitoring ports, two hosts, or one LAN only. **This is not a decision:** O-12 is the owner's. The node's design holds two sensors in its shape, a database each, and the third part builds one (#644 §1.2, `MaxSensors`; D5-10; D4's note 7 on #633). Its shape for two is option (ii).

| Option | C13 change | Cost |
| --- | --- | --- |
| (i) One host, two monitoring ports | None. The discard runs in the frame stage (SL-3) | One machine touches both LANs (SL-28, O-12). On the SEL-2730M and RSTP-mode switches the mirror target is documented as taking no ingress during a monitoring session (D2 on #627); O-1's rule still counts a mirror port as accepting ingress until an H-row shows otherwise for that model |
| (ii) Two hosts, two streams, no merge | None. Each sensor reports its LAN (`interfaces[].lan`, `prp.lans_seen` with one LAN), so `duplicates_discarded: false` is not a mismatch (0.1 §4.4) | Each event on both LANs can alert twice. The two streams also differ by design: a singly attached node's frames are on one LAN only (D2). Which sensor ties the node's own sessions is D5's |
| (iii) Two hosts, merged at the node | For each cited frame, its RCT sequence number and a hash with the LAN ID, tag and padding masked. For flow counts, either each flow's set of RCT sequence numbers, which is unbounded, or a rule that one LAN's sensor is primary | A merge stage in the node. The two clocks must agree well within the time a source takes to wrap its sequence number (A4), not within `prp_window_ms` (D2 corrects `674ce453`). Both backlogs must drain before a merge |
| (iv) One LAN only | None | One host and one port. Every doubly attached node's traffic is seen, because it sends each frame on both LANs (D2, inference). Singly attached nodes on the other LAN are not seen, and the coverage statement says one LAN |

**D4's view:** (iii) costs a merge stage for the gain of one alert instead of two. Use (i) where one host may touch both LANs, otherwise (ii) or (iv).

**Meets:** no SL rule. Brief item 7 and O-12.

**Test:** nothing to test. These are options, not requirements.

## 9. A blind sensor (brief item 8)

**CH-31. Per-interface counts and carrier in every heartbeat, and the node's rule.**

```
"interfaces": [ { "name": "<token>", "carrier": "up|down|unknown", "packets": <uint64> } ]
```

- **Input:** required in 0.2 heartbeats, at most `MaxInterfaces` (8), one entry for each interface in `coverage.interfaces`, by name.
- **The node's checks:**
  - A set of names that differs from the coverage statement's is refused as `conflict`, and so is a sum of `packets` that differs from `counters.packets`.
  - A falling `interfaces[].packets` within a run is refused as `order` by `Follows` (counted `counter-decrease`).
  - A `carrier` outside its set is refused as `form`.
- `packets` is the frames the frame stage read from the first pipe for that interface since the run began, before the PRP discard. The interface is the slot that each pipe record carries (SL-3). `carrier` is its link state; `unknown` when it cannot be read (assumption A2).
- **The node's rule** (difference 6, ruling; #628 §1.8):
  - An interface is **blind** when its `packets` has not risen across the heartbeats of the last `blind_after_s` of the node's monotonic time, or when its `carrier` is `down`.
  - Only heartbeats that are not late (CH-23) count.
  - `sensor-blind` is raised when **any** interface is blind, and names it. A coverage gap opens only when **every** interface is blind.
  - The comparison starts again at a new run's first heartbeat.
- **`blind_after_s`:** a node setting, 300 s by default, from 60 s to 3,600 s.
- **The basis (assumption A7):** a mirrored station LAN carries periodic traffic. D2 cites a device-side source per device (D2 on #627, from the manuals): a SEL device on PRP sends supervision frames every `PRPINTV` (1 to 10 s, default 2); GOOSE repeats at its maximum time; C37.238 PTP messages go every second. So 60 s of silence on a port that mirrors such a device is not normal. Whether a site's mirror carries those frames is per site, and is unshown, so the default is the wider one.
- **What this cannot see:** a sensor that lies about its counts (AP-36). The node's own-session check is the independent one.
- **Counted:** stretches of `sensor-blind`, under CH-45's cap.
- **Meets:** SL-20.
- **Test:** K-1, K-2, K-3.

**CH-44. What resets `sensor-unreachable`, and coverage that is unknown** (D3's note 2, ruling 3).

- **Input:** frames on a connection whose hello was accepted.
- **Outcome:**
  - Only an **accepted** frame resets `sensor-unreachable`'s timer: a tick accepted and not early (CH-8), or a heartbeat or record taken (CH-17). A refused record, a failed hello, an early tick or an unknown type does not reset it (AP-36 (2); D4-73's rule that a reconnect never resets it).
  - A stretch of 60 s with no heartbeat that is not late, while accepted ticks arrive, shows as **coverage unknown**. Status shows `coverage: unknown`, and the node raises **`sensor-no-heartbeat`** once a stretch. The name and the 60 s are #628 §1.8's.
  - **The interval of such a silence is a coverage gap, and the alarm is unchanged** (D3's item 6 for #645; SL-16's ninth cause; D6's B1 on #641). The alarm says the heartbeats stopped. The interval is carried by a `silent` gap, opened from the earlier of W and R once no current heartbeat has come for `HeartbeatGapMax` while the link is current and no gap is open (CH-45). *Test:* V-16.
  - A flat interface count seen only in late heartbeats is recorded, once the backlog drains, as a past blind stretch in one health event, `sensor.past-blind`, under CH-45's cap.
- **Counted:** each alarm's stretches, under CH-45's cap.
- **Meets:** SL-20, AP-36.
- **Test:** K-4; V-16 for the gap.

## 10. What the sensor states about itself

### 10.1 The configuration digest

**CH-32. `coverage.config_digest`, and in `sensor-hello`.** Required in 0.2: `sha256:` and 64 lowercase hex digits.

- **What it covers.** At the start of each run, the agent builds a manifest M: UTF-8 lines, each ended by `\n`, sorted by their bytes, with no duplicate:
  - `program <name> sha256:<hex>`, one for each of the four programs of SL-3: `socket-holder`, `frame-stage`, `engine` and `agent`. **[S2]** Which files make up the engine's executable and its plugins depends on the engine;
  - `file <path> sha256:<hex>` for every configuration file any of the four reads at start, and for the files that confine them. That includes:
    - the engine's scripts and packages (**[S2]**);
    - the allow-list (**[S2]** for its form);
    - the redaction configuration;
    - the capture configuration and the agent's configuration;
    - **the socket holder's call-filter profile** (SL-3);
    - **the egress rule set** of SL-26.
    - `<path>` is relative to the sensor's configuration root, matches `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*$`, is at most 255 bytes, and has no `..` segment.
    - The agent's key and its enrolment are not in M. They are not configuration, and a renewed enrolment must not read as an edit;
  - `setting <name> <decimal>` for `prp_window_ms`, `prp_table_entries`, `sa_reply_timeout_ms`, `max_pending_dnp3`, `flow_idle_s`, `buffer_bytes`, `buffer_sync_ms` and `ring_bytes`. `ring_bytes` is the capture ring's size in bytes, 0 allowed (O-11);
  - `redaction <slot> on|off` for each slot of D4-67 and D4-68, under these closed names: `telnet-password`, `ftp-pass`, `snmp-community`, `http-basic`, `http-cookie`, `http-set-cookie`, `http-form-body`. A server's echo of a slot is part of its slot.
- `config_digest` is `sha256:` and the hex SHA-256 of M.
- **It is constant within a run.** Each program reads its configuration only at its start, and any start begins a new run (CH-12). The node keeps the run's digest from its hello **in the cursor, saved at CH-13 row 6** (CH-18; SL-31; D3's preference, taken by #644 §1.4), so a `sensorrun.json` that is lost or behind cannot lose it or bring back an earlier run's. A record whose `config_digest` differs from it is refused as `conflict`, across a node restart too (D5's note on #627). **With the sensor database lost,** the digest is learned again from the next hello, and status says so. It is the sensor's word either way, and row 8 still compares it with the configured digest.
- **A site build has no `off`.** The site's frame stage, the program that redacts (SL-3), refuses at start a configuration that names the switch (SL-22, D1's ruling on #624). So `redaction <slot> off` appears only in a test build.
- **The agent hashes the files on disk at the run's start,** not what each program loaded. It shows an edit made through the files. A program that loaded something else is not shown (D3's note 12).
- **For the egress rule set this limit is stronger.** The digest hashes the rule file on disk, not the rules the kernel holds. Root loads those rules, and can change them without touching the file. So the digest shows an edit of the file and nothing else. `egress_check` (CH-27), where it can be read, and SL-T21 are what speak to the kernel's rules.
- **The agent shows M at its console,** so that a person can compare it with the digest the node shows.
- **The expected digest** is `network_sensor.sensors[].config_digest` in the node's configuration, read from the sensor's console at commissioning, as the pins are (D5-4, #628 §1.15). A hello or record whose digest is not the expected one raises `sensor-coverage-mismatch`. It never refuses a hello and never fails a start (CH-13 row 8). Each patch of the sensor therefore needs an edit of the node's configuration, and until then the alarm stands.
- **What it shows, and what it does not.** It is the sensor's own word (AP-36). It shows an edit made through the sensor's own programs, such as a redaction slot switched off or an allow-list widened. It does not show a compromise.
- **Counted:** `conflict` refusals; stretches of the mismatch alarm.
- **Meets:** SL-3, SL-19, SL-22, SL-26.
- **Test:** G-1, G-2, G-3.

### 10.2 How the sensor's end was pinned

**CH-33. `pinned_by` in `sensor-hello`.**

- **Input:** the closed field: `signature` when the agent verified the enrolment's admin signature (SL-5 step 6), `console` when an installer typed the node's pin at the sensor's console (SL-5's fallback).
- **Outcome:**
  - Another value closes the connection (`hello`).
  - The node keeps the first value of each generation durably (CH-18). A different value later in the same generation closes the connection (`binding`, with C8), because the way an end is pinned changes only with a new enrolment. D3 adds the reason to C8 0.13 (D3's note 7).
  - The node shows it in status, and raises the standing alarm `sensor-pinned-by-console` while a live sensor reports `console`. There is no refusal (ruling 4 on #628; #628 §1.3).
- **Counted:** `binding` closes.
- **Meets:** SL-5.
- **Test:** G-4.

### 10.3 The allow-list's count

**CH-34. `engine_lines_refused`,** a heartbeat counter: a run total that never decreases, required in 0.2.

- **Input:** each line the link agent takes from the engine.
- **Outcome:** a line from a stream not on the allow-list, or one that fails the agent's strict parse, is refused, wiped, never buffered and never sent (SL-22). A field not on the list is not taken and not counted, because the line it came in is still used.
- **Counted:** `engine_lines_refused`.
- **[S2]** What a "line" is depends on the engine's output form: one log line, or one JSON object.
- **Meets:** SL-22.
- **Test:** G-5.

### 10.4 The agent's own counts

**CH-43. The agent's counts are in every heartbeat** (D6's CH-3 row: "counted only at a console with no defined form").

```
"agent": { "closes": { "handshake": <uint64>, "node-hello": <uint64>, "node-hello-timeout": <uint64>, "binding": <uint64>,
                       "ack": <uint64>, "type": <uint64>, "idle": <uint64>, "frame-too-large": <uint64>,
                       "superseded": <uint64>, "run-complete": <uint64>, "shutdown": <uint64> },
           "taken_ahead": <uint64>, "sync_late": <uint64>, "entries_unreadable": <uint64> }
```

- **Input:** required in 0.2 heartbeats, with every key present: the closed set of CH-11's agent reasons, and CH-6's and CH-19's counts. Each is a run total.
- **Outcome:** a missing or unknown key is refused (`decode` or `missing`). A falling value within a run is refused as `order` by `Follows`.
- **Counted:** the node shows the latest values in status. **Their rises are not in `sensor.counters`** (changed under P4-4-L6, for C4 0.4; D5's B6 on #634). With the agent's 14 counts added, the worst `sensor.counters` event renders in 2,316 octets, above a default Syslog target's 2,048; without them, in 1,569: an unreproduced estimate from one local render, which C4 0.4's E4-6 pins in CI when the code lands (`docs/phase4/D4-c4-0.4-proposal.md`, §4). The agent's counts are diagnostics of the link. Its losses already reach `sensor.counters` through `drops_channel`, which counts `entries_unreadable` too (CH-19).
- **Meets:** SL-19 (what the agent refuses, made visible), SL-20.
- **Test:** M-5, A-1, B-3, H-4.

## 11. The 0.1 reader, and every new field

**CH-35. Every change bumps the minor and keeps a 0.1 reader.**

- **The channel stays strict.** A 0.2 node reads 0.2 on the channel only (CH-36). A 0.1 peer cannot pass the hello (`hello`), which is right: 0.1 has no binding, no resume and no acknowledgement.
- **The reader for stored records accepts both identifiers.** It reads any 0.x minor already: strictly for its own and earlier minors, and forward for a later one (`DecodeStored`, `storedSchema`).
  - A field that 0.2 requires in a channel record is optional in the Go type, required in a 0.2 channel record, and may be absent in a stored record of any minor.
  - **An alert's two new coverage fields are optional at every minor** (D6), because a 0.2 node can raise an alert from a stored 0.1 observation, whose coverage lacks them.

| New in 0.2 | Where | A 0.1 reader | The 0.2 reader on a 0.1 record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The four messages | The channel only, never stored | Never meets them | — |
| `made_utc`; `node.late`, `node.backlog`, `node.skewed`, `node.lost` | Stored observation | Ignored (`DecodeStored` reads a later minor forward) | Absent: not known, not late, not marked |
| `coverage.config_digest`, `coverage.settings.prp_window_ms` | Observation and heartbeat; stored with the observation; flattened into an alert | Stored observation: ignored. Alert: two unknown details, accepted from a later minor (`AlertFromHealth`) | Absent: not known |
| `asset_id` `clock-skew`, `sensor-records-late` and `hold-timeout`; details `assets` and `assets_total` (CH-47) | Alert | **Refused**, because `Alert.Validate` accepts only an asset ID or `asset-unresolved` (`alert.go`). The details alone would be accepted from a later minor. The bridge build accepts the three values | — |
| `current` in `sensor-hello` | The channel only | Never meets it | — |
| `interfaces`, `agent`, and the five new heartbeat counters of the discard and the agent | Heartbeat, never stored as a record | The rises of the discard's counters go in the capped `sensor.counters` (CH-45; C4 0.4); the agent's counts are shown in status only and are not in `sensor.counters` (CH-43) | — |
| The socket holder's counters `drops_holder`, `frames_overlong` and `frames_outgoing` (CH-26), and the field `egress_check` (CH-27) | Heartbeat, never stored as a record. A 0.2 node reads them; `egress_check` may be absent | Never meets them | — |
| The detector's checkpoint | `netcheckpoint.detector` | **Passed over** (below) | Read: a 0.1 checkpoint's coverage lacks the two fields |

**The rollback** (D5's finding, D6 B1; D4 confirmed the reading of the code; ruling on the 0.1 rollback):

- **What `main` does.** `Runtime.detectorCheckpoints` (`platform/node/netdetector.go`) keeps only documents whose `schema` equals `otsec.netcheckpoint/0.1`. So a 0.1 node does not see a 0.2 checkpoint at all. It starts as a first start, with no `network-detector-not-restored`. It judges the retained observations again and stores their alerts a second time. It never removes the 0.2 document, which can be up to 1,354,752 bytes in the configuration class.
- **Required: a bridge build, released before the first build that writes 0.2.** Its node, still writing 0.1:
  1. recognises any `otsec.netcheckpoint/0.x` detector document;
  2. for a minor it cannot read, raises `network-detector-not-restored`, starts its detector from the oldest retained observation, and removes that document once its own first checkpoint has committed. **It raises the retained observations' alerts again, and says so with the alarm.** That is a stated limit (D6; #628 §1.15 item 3, where D5 keeps it rather than read an unverified `last_alert_seq`);
  3. accepts an alert of a later minor whose `asset_id` is `clock-skew`, `sensor-records-late` or `hold-timeout`;
  4. reads an unknown `coverage.degraded` value of a later minor as degraded, not as a refusal, so that a later minor can add one (D6's note on round two);
  5. ties a held observation by patching its raw JSON rather than re-encoding it with 0.1's type, so that a held 0.2 body keeps `made_utc` and its `node` marks (#628 §1.15 item 2).
- **It is a release rule** (ruling 14 on #628): D7 records the bridge build in `docs/release.md` as a release floor, with the configuration shape that rolls back with it (#628 §1.15 item 4).
- **Rollback from 0.2 is supported only to the bridge build.** A rollback to an older build gets the silent restart above: duplicate alerts, and an orphaned checkpoint. The third part's brief carries the build order.
- **The learner's checkpoint** has no coverage statement, and its form does not change. It takes the new identifier because both halves share one (0.1 §8), and the bridge build's reader accepts both.
- **The intake hold's checkpoint** keeps `otsec.netcheckpoint.intake/0.1`. A held body is raw JSON decoded with `json.Unmarshal`, so a 0.2 body passes (D5-9).
- **Sizes.**
  - The two coverage fields take about 110 bytes, inside `MaxCoverageBytes` (2 KiB), which does not change.
  - `made_utc` and the node marks add up to about 60 bytes to a stored observation, plus `node.lost` at most 8 spans of about 70 bytes.
  - The two flattened coverage details add about 130 bytes to an alert, and `assets` and `assets_total` at most about 330.
  - The source cursor adds up to the node's 4,096 bytes to `Record.Bytes`: at most 930 with no span or `prior` held, and up to 2,026 while they are held (CH-18; 192 before P4-4-L6).
  - **The worst stored body is pinned against 0.1's `MaxNetobsBytes` (24 KiB),** because a 0.1 `DecodeStored` checks that first (D6). If the worst-case tests fail with the new fields, the bound goes to D1, and is not changed silently. No test for this proposal was run in CI or committed; the one local render behind CH-43's and C4 0.4's octet figures is not reproducible and is not evidence of any class (C4 0.4 §4).
- **Meets:** no SL rule. The brief's acceptance.
- **Test:** R-1 to R-6. R-3's fixture and test land on `main` before the 0.2 code, while the 0.1 code is there to run them (D6).

## 12. Bounds

New in 0.2, beside 0.1 §10, which is unchanged. "Unmeasured" means that the basis is a reason and not a measurement.

| Name | Value | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MaxNodeMessage` | 1 KiB | The node's two messages are under 300 bytes (SL-19) |
| `HandshakeTimeout` | 10 s | D4's choice for a site LAN (SL-12); unmeasured |
| `HelloTimeout` | 10 s | One `SensorTickInterval` |
| `IdleTimeout` | 30 s | Three missed ticks or acks |
| `SensorTickInterval` | 10 s | The heartbeat's interval in 0.1. Not the node's `TickInterval` (60 s, `service.go`) |
| `TickMinSpacing` | 5 s | Half the interval: a faster tick is counted and does nothing (difference 10) |
| `AckMaxDelay` | 1 s after the saved T moves; an `ack` at least every 10 s | Unmeasured. The node's commits can be slow: F-016 recorded store commits held for 3 s, and up to 37.9 s, on the hosted Windows runner (D6). So the sensor's unacknowledged backlog on a live link is the commit time plus 1 s |
| `RedialFloor` | 10 s | CH-38; both documents, by D1's ruling |
| `buffer_bytes` | 2 GiB; floor 256 MiB in a site build and 16 MiB in a test build; at most 64 GiB | CH-20. A sizing rule against `outage_hours` that cannot be checked yet |
| `buffer_sync_ms` | 100 ms | The most records an agent or host crash loses (CH-19, CH-42); unmeasured on any sensor hardware. A slower sync is counted (`sync_late`) |
| `MaxAgentRuns` | 64 | CH-46 |
| `MaxLostSpans` | 8 | CH-40 |
| `late_bound_s` | 180 s, from 30 s to 3,600 s | CH-23; both documents, by D1's ruling |
| `backlog_tie_s` | 60 s, at most 120 s | CH-39; both documents, by D1's ruling (D5 drops `TieBreakBound`) |
| `max_skew_ms` | 2,000 ms raise, 1,500 ms clear; the least o of 6 ticks | CH-22, #628 §1.5; unmeasured |
| `blind_after_s` | 300 s, from 60 s to 3,600 s | CH-31, #628 §1.8; A7 |
| `prp_window_ms` | 400 ms, from 10 ms to 2,000 ms | CH-28; A4 |
| `prp_table_entries` | 65,536, from 4 to 1,048,576 | CH-29; rate times window: at 400 ms it holds about 163,000 frames a second (D2); unmeasured |
| `MaxInterfaces` | 8 (0.1) | Now also bounds `interfaces` in the heartbeat |
| Sensor health events | **45 an hour** for each sensor: 12 `intake.drop-counted`; 4 `sensor.coverage-gap` with cause `lost` and 4 of every other cause, each on its own budget; a pool of 24, in which each name is at most 4 an hour and `sensor.counters` also comes no sooner than 10 minutes after its last; 1 mode change. 3 for a retired sensor. At most 4,096 bytes each. 41 before P4-4-L6 | CH-45; #644 §1.9 item 5 and §1.10 |
| `HeartbeatGapMax` | 30 s between consecutive heartbeats of a chain by their `at_utc`, its ends included; and at least the gap's length on the node's clock ÷ 30 s heartbeats in the chain | CH-39: three `SensorTickInterval`s at the 0.2 heartbeat's cadence; one value in C13, #644 §1.10 and SL-16 |
| `MaxLinkCursorBytes` | 4,096 bytes (D1's ruling on #645, comment 6846045180; 2,048 before it), for a text form of at most 2,026 with the `drops_driver` baseline, what is held with W, the gap held for the floor and M (192 for 187 before P4-4-L6) | CH-18; the node's constant, stated here so both documents give one figure (#644 §1.4, §1.10) |
| A `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap written `open` | After it has stood 60 s from W, or 60 s after its open on the monotonic clock where that is earlier, as `sensor-unreachable` is due | CH-45; #644 §1.9 item 5 |
| W saved on its own | At most once a minute | CH-17, CH-45 |
| Coverage unknown | 60 s of accepted ticks with no heartbeat that is not late | CH-44; #628 §1.8 |
| Dials before a first accepted hello | 3 for each address-and-pin pair, each a connection attempt of at most 5 s | CH-1, CH-38; SL-30 |
| `drops_holder`, `frames_overlong`, `frames_outgoing` | Run totals, each at most 2^64 − 1, never decreasing within a run (`Follows`) | CH-26 |
| The socket holder's buffer | At least the monitoring interface's largest frame with its tags; a longer frame is counted under `frames_overlong` | CH-26; SL-3. The frame stage still cuts at `MaxFrameBytes` (0.1 §10) |
| `egress_check` | `ok` or `absent`; left out when it cannot be read without a capability | CH-27 |

## 13. The node's side, as #628 answers it

D1 ruled that D5's answers are written into C13's text before approval (ruling 7). Each is now a clause or a stated limit:

| | Point | D5's answer (#628 §1.15), and the ruling | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D5-1 | Which outcomes are final | As proposed: a C13 refusal; a drop by the budget, at a full class or at the record cap. Any other store error closes as `store` | CH-17, CH-11 |
| D5-2 | Drop counts durable before the `ack` passes them | The run's refused and dropped totals in the source cursor (#628 §1.4; ruling 2; D4's B1 on #628). The cursor was at most 192 bytes in all; under P4-4-L6 it also holds what changes with the run, and is at most 4,096 (CH-18; #644 §1.4; D1's ruling on #645, comment 6846045180). At `a50ee61c`, #628 used an `intake.drop-counted` event instead | CH-17, CH-18 |
| D5-3 | What the node keeps, G and D | All of it | CH-18 |
| D5-4 | The expected digest | `network_sensor.sensors[].config_digest`; an alarm, never a refusal | CH-32 |
| D5-5 | `pinned_by` | Shown, the first of each generation kept; `sensor-pinned-by-console` and no refusal (ruling 4 on #628) | CH-33 |
| D5-6 | The dialer | As CH-4, with a 5 s connect and a 10 s write deadline; redial under CH-38 | CH-4, CH-38 |
| D5-7 | The backlog and the hold | The late index (difference 1), and no tie after a long break (ruling 1) | CH-39 |
| D5-8 | `sensor-unreachable` | Only an accepted frame resets it (ruling 3) | CH-44 |
| D5-9 | Health events from the heartbeat | None for each heartbeat; capped `sensor.counters` and `sensor.run-loss` (difference 12) | CH-45 |
| D5-10 | One sensor or several | Two in the shape, a database each, and one built: CH-30's option (ii) | CH-30 |
| D5-11 | Names, bounds, caps | As below | CH-45 |
| D5-12 | A two-way `ServeSensor` | Built as #628 §1.1 says | CH-25 |

**CH-45. The node's sensor alarms and events, and their caps** (#628 §1.9 item 5 and §1.10). **One list in both documents** (ruling 4 on #634): CH-45's pool and the node design's list hold the same names, the node-only ones included.

Each alarm below is a status alarm once a stretch, unless the row says otherwise.

**The health-event budget, for each sensor, an hour** (#644 §1.9 item 5 and §1.10; its §1.11 row 12):

| Budget | Records an hour |
| --- | --- |
| `intake.drop-counted`, for two classes, at most once every 10 minutes each | 12 |
| `sensor.coverage-gap` with cause `lost`. **A budget of its own, so that link drops cannot delay a declared loss** (D4-90's gap rule; D4's B4 on #633) | 4 |
| `sensor.coverage-gap` of every other cause. A budget of its own, so a coverage gap never waits behind another event (D6) | 4 |
| A pool for every other sensor event in the table below, the node-only names included. **Each name is at most 4 an hour, and `sensor.counters` also comes no sooner than 10 minutes after its last** (D5's N6 on #634: the 4 an hour binds `sensor.counters` too, and the 10 minutes spaces it). The pool states which event waits when it is spent | 24 |
| The sensor database's mode change | 1 |
| **In all** | **45** (3 for a retired sensor). It was 41 before P4-4-L6 |

Past a cap, events are merged and carried in the next record. **When the pool is spent, what waits** (#644 §1.9 item 5): `sensor.counters` first, then `sensor.run-loss`, then the alarms' events. `sensor.redial-granted` and `sensor.revocation-applied` wait last. A waiting pool event is held in `sensorrun.json`, merged into the next of its name, counted in `health_held`, and written first when room returns. **A coverage gap is never held in `sensorrun.json`** (below).

| Alarm or event | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| `sensor-clock-skew` | CH-22 |
| `sensor-blind` | CH-31 |
| `sensor-no-heartbeat` (coverage unknown; #628 §1.8's name) | CH-44 |
| `sensor.past-blind` (a health event, no alarm) | CH-44 |
| `sensor-gap-undeclared`: the alarm once for each rise of the excess in a run (#628 §1.4) | CH-13, CH-15 |
| `sensor-records-late`: the alarm once a connection | CH-23 |
| `sensor-records-lost`, with a `lost` coverage gap | CH-40, CH-46 |
| `sensor-buffer-filling`, at three quarters of `buffer_bytes` | CH-20 |
| `sensor-buffer-small`, standing | CH-13, CH-20 |
| `sensor-coverage-mismatch`: the run's `config_digest` differs from the configured one. **It has this meaning only in 0.2** (CH-32); the pipe era's comparison of the coverage statement with expected interfaces is gone (D5's N5 on #634, D4's note on #633: #644's Part 2 §5 is D5's to bring in line) | CH-13, CH-32 |
| `sensor-pinned-by-console`, standing | CH-33 |
| `sensor-identity` (#628 §1.9 item 6) | CH-14 |
| `sensor-egress-absent`, while a heartbeat states `egress_check` `absent`, when the field is dropped within a run, and when the node holds no baseline for the run and a heartbeat that is not the run's first seen from its start omits it: an alarm, never a refusal | CH-27 |
| `sensor-transmitted`, standing until an administrator clears it (the clear is recorded by this event, written with the alarm cleared, and the alarm drops only after it commits; #644 §1.8, `TestTransmittedClear`): at a non-zero `frames_outgoing` in a heartbeat taken with no baseline held for the run, or any rise against the baseline, late heartbeats included; and at a `sensorrun.json` lost, unreadable or behind while an enrolment is live, or one that cannot be compared with the sensor's database (CH-18) (ruling 3 on #634; D5's B1 on #634; D1's alarm half; CH-26). Never a refusal, and never a proof of none | CH-26, CH-18 |
| `sensor-dial-limit`, standing, after SL-30's three dials, or with the count read as spent. Its health event goes in the pool under its cap | CH-1, CH-38 |
| `sensor.counters`, carrying the rises of the heartbeat's counters as C4 0.4 lists them, and not the agent's counts (CH-43). **It names a rise of `drops_driver`, `drops_holder` or `frames_overlong` as a capture loss,** in its `capture_loss` detail (C4 0.4; D1's ruling 1 in comment 6840581561 on #645 that `capture_loss` includes `drops_driver`, since more loss counted fails closed; ruling 5 on #634 names `frames_overlong` a capture loss beside `drops_holder`) | CH-15, CH-26, CH-29 |
| `sensor.run-loss`, for each new run that declares a loss or an unclean end, and the node's own count on `replace`. **It carries the run's generation, counter and `run_id`** (CH-16) | CH-12, CH-16, CH-42 |
| `sensor-state-unread`, when the node's sensor state, a position or the run file cannot be read (formerly `sensor-state-unknown` here). **One name for four causes that act differently, so status and its event carry the cause as the detail `cause`** (D5's N3 on #634; #644 §1.3 and its §1.11 row 6): `fold-record`, a record in the sensor fold that cannot be parsed (every open sensor connection closes as `state-unread`, and none is dialled, CH-11); `fold-read`, the fold's read failed (the links this run confirmed by a full fold are kept, no other is dialled, and the read is retried); `position`, a sensor database or its cursor cannot be read (that sensor is not dialled, CH-4); `run-file`, `sensorrun.json` unreadable, lost or behind, or unable to be compared with a sensor's database that is lost, absent, unread or without a cursor, which reads as lost for every value of that sensor, `sensor-transmitted` included, but for the copy of the earlier of W and R and the run copy `node-hello` names (CH-18) (the node dials on the cursor's proof, the count is spent otherwise, and `sensor-transmitted` is raised, CH-18) | CH-4, CH-11, CH-18 |
| **Node-only names, in the same pool** (#628 §1.9 item 5). A sensor cannot drive these. They are listed here so that both documents hold one list:<br>• the alarms `sensor-link-key` (the link key's file, #628 §1.9 item 11), `sensor-enrolment-expiring` and `sensor-enrolment-expired` (SL-8; #628 §1.3), `network-detector-lag` (#628 §1.9 item 3) and `network-session-not-tied` (#628 §1.9 item 4);<br>• the events `sensor.redial-granted` (an administrator's re-arm, #628 §1.1) and `sensor.revocation-applied` (#628 §1.3);<br>• the sensor database's store alarms | — |

- **The ceiling is a byte figure** (ruling 9 on #628; #628 §1.9 item 5).
  - Each health record is at most 4,096 bytes.
  - Collection's need over `outage_hours`, C, is reserved first.
  - A sensor event is written only while the platform class holds less than its quota less C after it, so a sensor can never fill the class that `collection.missed` needs.
  - A pool event held past the ceiling is kept in `sensorrun.json`, written at most once a minute when its counts change, and written once room returns. **It is held as its counts and facts and built at its write, never as built event bytes,** so a binary rolled back to C4 0.3 code builds it with its own identifier and does not refuse it (C4 0.4 §3; #644 §1.9 item 5; to be tested by `TestHeldPoolEventBuiltAtWrite`, E4-7's node leg). A crash can lose up to the last minute's held counts, and a `sensorrun.json` lost or behind names them in status as possibly lost (CH-18; #644 §1.9 item 5). **A held coverage gap is held in the cursor:** a `lost` gap in its span's owed mark (CH-40), and every other gap by W, which does not move past it, or, across the raise at an unwatched close, in the cursor's gap held for the floor (CH-18; below).
- **The sensor alarms that write no health event** (D5's N7 on #634; #644 §1.9 item 5 and its §1.11 row 14). These are status and the operating system's log only, as `Status.Alarm` writes on `main`: `sensor-unreachable`, `sensor-position-not-saved`, `sensor-failed`, `sensor-state-not-durable`, `sensor-revoke-pending`, `sensor-not-enrolled`, `sensor-address-is-asset`, `sensor-platform-floor`, `sensor-store-not-open`, `sensor-intake-not-started`, `sensor-late-index-short` and `sensor-not-built`. Every interval they leave unwatched is a coverage gap (below), and the gap is a health event.
- **This replaces 0.1 §6's** "the node writes the differences as a health event at most once a minute" with the capped `sensor.counters`. Heartbeats themselves never become events (difference 12).
- `sensor.link_refused` is capped for each sensor, generation and reason, once in a stretch and once an hour, with the hour starting again at each succeeded `sensor.enrolled` of that sensor. **A stretch ends at the next accepted hello, and at a node start** (C8-5; ruling 2 on #631; #644 §1.9 item 6; CH-1).

**Coverage gaps** (D4-90's gap rule; SL-16 and C8-7 in #641; #644 §1.9 item 5 and its §1.11 row 11; D4's B4 on #633). **Every interval the platform did not watch is a gap, on the node's clock, whatever caused it, and the rule fails closed:** what the node cannot show watched is recorded as unwatched. This replaces 0.2's earlier rule, under which only `lost` and `blind` were gaps and a link drop showed as `coverage: unknown` with no record. It also replaces D1's first text on #639, which said a link drop and the node's start are not gaps, and which D6 found failed open (B1 on #639); D4-90 records the replacement.

- **The causes, one closed set,** the same nine tokens in C13, the node's design (#644 §1.9 item 5), SL-16 and C8-7. The ninth, `silent`, is new at this revision; SL-16 leaves its token to CH-45 (D3's item 1 for #645), and CH-45 takes `silent` as D5 proposed it (comment 6840288076 on #645; #644 §1.9 item 5). It names the gap, not the alarm: `sensor-no-heartbeat` (CH-44) stays as it is:

  | Cause | The node opens it |
  | --- | --- |
  | `node-start` | At its start, for each configured sensor, from the earlier of W and R (below). It takes the node's own downtime and whatever the sensor did meanwhile, so a station power loss that stopped both machines leaves no interval in no gap |
  | `link` | When a connection whose hello was accepted ends, or a dial fails, while no gap is open: from the earlier of W and R. It takes the sensor host down, a network fault and a sensor never back. **And while the node holds no W for a sensor, from the live enrolment's `enrolled_at`** (SL-16 and gap leg 15; D3's ruling (b), comment 6839793790 on #644; D5's B1 on #645): a sensor never watched since its enrolment holds this gap from its enrolment, whether or not a connection has ended or a dial has failed, with cause `link`, or `node-start` when the node finds it at a start. It is written as any `link` gap is: `open` once it has stood 60 s and again at its close, or once, `closed`, if it closes sooner. It closes at the first current heartbeat that shows capture, always with `covered: false` (CH-39), and carries `from_bound: true` with `from_utc` = `enrolled_at` |
  | `restart` | Never at the open. A `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap becomes `restart` when the node reads, before the gap closes, the hello of a run that began inside it. It takes a sensor restart during an outage. **The sensor host down** shows as `link` while it lasts and as `restart` once the next run's hello is read, because the node cannot tell it from a network fault by itself |
  | `held` | From the earlier of W and R, when the node stops dialling a sensor, or closes its link, on a decision of its own other than a revocation: a fork hold, the dial limit, the state or position unread (CH-11's `state-unread` included), the store not open, the intake not started, the link key, a pending revocation file, an enrolment the configuration does not match or that has expired, an asset's address, the platform floor, and a `renew`'s close until the sensor's end holds it (CH-37) |
  | `revoked` | From the earlier of W and R, at a revocation's close (CH-37) |
  | `sensor-failed` | From the earlier of W and R, when a recovered panic stops the sensor's work until a restart (#644 §1.9 item 9; D3's addition) |
  | `silent` | **From the earlier of W and R, when no current heartbeat has come for `HeartbeatGapMax` (30 s) on the node's monotonic clock, counted from the receipt of W's heartbeat, while the link is current and no gap is open** (SL-16's ninth cause; D6's B1 on #641; D3's item 1 for #645). Ticks are not buffered and keep the link inside `IdleTimeout`, so a wedged agent or buffer can stop the heartbeats with no connection's end, and without this cause the next current heartbeat would move W across the silence. It is written as a `link` gap is: `open` once it has stood 60 s from W, or 60 s after it opened on the node's monotonic clock where that is earlier ("When it is written", below), and again at its close, or once, `closed`, if it closes sooner. **It closes as covered only by the chain** (CH-39), and a stall leaves it unwatched. Its cause ends at the first current heartbeat whose lateness (CH-23) is under `HeartbeatGapMax`, so heartbeats only delayed behind a flood close it as covered once they arrive on time (D6's N1 on #641 at `467c1dd6`). When it and a connection's end fall due at the same evaluation, one gap opens, with cause `link`; when it opens first, the end adds `link` to `causes`. Opening at 30 s costs nothing in the stream, since a gap closed as covered within 60 s writes nothing. CH-44's `sensor-no-heartbeat` is unchanged. *Test:* V-16 |
  | `lost` | For each new or widened span (CH-40, SL-31) |
  | `blind` | When every interface is blind (CH-31), from the earlier of W and R (below) |

- **W, the moment accounted through,** kept in the cursor (CH-18): the node's own time through which every interval was either watched or written in a committed gap event.
  - While no gap is open, **W moves, at each current heartbeat, to the earlier of the node's receipt of it and its `at_utc` + E** (CH-22's E as it stands at the receipt, its samples moved back by any step back found since each was taken, under the step rule below; SL-16; D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`), never below the floor (a value below it is taken at it) and never later than the node's wall clock (below). The heartbeat is one read on a connection whose hello was accepted, of the run that is current (CH-7) once the backlog is through (CH-39), not late (CH-23), and **showing capture by CH-31's blind rule, flat counts included: not every interface blind under CH-31 at its receipt,** that is, some interface has a `carrier` that is not `down` and a `packets` that has risen within the last `blind_after_s` of the node's monotonic time, or whose run began within it, since CH-31 starts its comparison again at a run's first heartbeat (D3's item 2 for #645; SL-16; D6's B2 on #641; D6's B1 on #641 at `8f78d057`). Never the carrier alone. When every interface is blind, W stays, and the `blind` gap opens from the earlier of W and R. That receipt is a moment the node saw itself (D3's condition 1 in SL-16). A time the sensor states is not one, **the heartbeat's own `at_utc` included, however close it is to the receipt** (D6's B3 on #641; *test:* V-1's second case, whose control keeps `at_utc` as W and starts the gap five minutes late), and nor is a backlog record's commit time. **`at_utc` + E serves only to take W, or R, below the receipt,** never above it; with the sensor clock five minutes ahead, E cancels the offset. The node keeps the heartbeat's `seq` and `at_utc` with W, for the chain (CH-39), and `at_utc` serves the chain and that bound only.
  - **Why the bound** (SL-16; D6's B2 on #641 at `da7a84ee`). A heartbeat stays current up to `late_bound_s` late (CH-23). Heartbeats queue in the agent's buffer and ticks do not, so a slow intake can deliver every heartbeat about two minutes late without the ninth cause falling due. Placed at their receipts, W and R would lie that much after the moment their heartbeat shows, and that interval would be in no gap. **The residual** is the delay E itself takes in: the least, over the last six ticks, of the network delay and the queue in the stream (CH-22). **A sensor that lies gains nothing new.** It sets `at_utc` + E, but the bound only takes W and R below the receipt. So it can move them only earlier, which widens a gap, or decline the bound by stating a late `at_utc`, which gives back that delay and no more than stating heartbeats that show capture already gives it (AP-36). W and R take each new value even when it is earlier than the one before, since E can fall, and a gap starts at the earlier of the two either way. **The one limit is the floor** (below): no move back takes W or R below the end of a gap closed unwatched, since a move below it would start a later gap inside that gap, and a sensor that states early `at_utc`s could do it at will. *Test:* V-16's fifth case and V-17's ninth case.
  - **W does not move past an open gap** until that gap's closing event has committed or the gap is closed as covered, except at an unwatched close, whose raise is saved with the gap held for the floor (CH-18) before its event commits (below).
  - W is saved with every cursor save, and on its own at most once a minute when nothing commits (CH-17). So the gap after a start may begin up to a minute before the last heartbeat read: it reaches back **no later than** that heartbeat (D5's N4 on #641), which errs toward a longer gap.
  - With W the cursor keeps, from W's heartbeat, each interface's `packets` and the `at_utc` of its last rise in the run (or of the run's first heartbeat, for an interface that never rose), and R: the last current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose with that interface's `carrier` not `down`, placed on the node's clock as W is, never below the floor and never later than the node's wall clock. All are in the same writes as W. **While a gap is open, R does not move, as W does not.**
  - **A copy of the earliest of W, R and the `from_utc` of the gap held for the floor is kept in `sensorrun.json`,** written only with the file's own writes, as the node holds them when it writes the file, the gap held from the raise itself; while a gap is held for the floor the copy never rises (CH-18; below). It serves only when the cursor holds no W.
  - **On a step back of the wall clock seen while the node runs** (the step rule, below; D6's N2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`, D6's B1 on #641 at `02773aa1`), the node moves W, R, the floor, the copy and E's held samples back by the step, saves them at once, marks the gaps it affects `clock_stepped`, and writes `time.node-stepped` once. *Test:* V-9's fourth, fifth and eighth to eleventh cases, V-14's fifth and seventh cases and V-16's eighth case.
- **Where a gap starts** (SL-16; D6's B3 on #641 at `9d74bb73`, B2 at `467c1dd6`, B1 and N2 at `da7a84ee`, B1 at `8f78d057`). **Every gap the node opens, while it runs or at its start, starts at the earlier of W and R,** whatever its cause and whenever the node notices the cause. A sensor host that loses power sends no FIN, so the node sees the loss only at `IdleTimeout` (30 s, CH-4); a gap opened at the close would leave that 30 s, and up to a heartbeat interval before it, in no gap. W can run up to `blind_after_s` ahead of R, so a flat stretch that began before W began at R. R does not move while a gap is open, so the start is fixed at the open, and a gap that never closes is written `open` from it. **Two exceptions:** a `lost` gap is its span (CH-40), which can begin after W, and is written with its span's times; and a gap opened while the node holds no W starts at the bound (below: the run file's copy of the earlier of W and R, or `enrolled_at`), with `from_bound: true`.

  **The floor.** When a gap other than `lost` closes unwatched (`covered: false`), W and R become its `to_utc` wherever they are earlier or have no value, and the floor becomes that `to_utc` whatever it was before; after that neither W nor R takes a value below it (a new W or R below it is taken at it), the move back of a falling E included. It applies only at an unwatched close. So no gap other than `lost`, and other than one that starts at a bound, starts before the `to_utc` of an earlier gap closed unwatched, where no step back of the node's wall clock lies between the two gaps; without the floor, a later gap could start at an earlier gap's start and, closed as covered, claim with the same `from_utc` an interval already written unwatched. On a run flat from its first heartbeat with no R, the gap before it closes unwatched at that heartbeat, so the floor gives R a value and the `blind` gap starts no later than that gap's end. The floor is held in memory, and W and R are saved at or above it, so the floor adds no field; at a node start it is where the `node-start` gap begins, and the step check at that gap's close caps it. A step back of the wall clock moves it back with W, R and the copy.

  **W, R and the floor are never later than the node's wall clock** (D6's B1 on #641 at `cebfff61`). **The step rule** (SL-16 at #641's `0ed3138c`; D1's direction on D6's B1 on #641 at `02773aa1`). Before the node takes a time from its wall clock for a gap (its open, its close, its `from_utc` or `to_utc`, the floor's raise, a `lost` gap's cut end, the time a never-watched gap starts from), for W, R or the floor (a heartbeat's receipt, and the ticks' receipts that E is taken from), or for the run file's copy, it runs the step check, and it runs it at its tick as well. The check compares the wall clock with the monotonic clock since the check before. **It acts only on a step back of more than `max_skew_ms` (2,000 ms) between two checks;** a smaller difference moves nothing and marks nothing, so a time service that slews the clock does not mark every gap open through the slew (D6's N3 on #641 at `6723bf90`). A step back found moves W, R, the floor, the copy and E's held samples back by the step. **The floor in this rule is the gap floor** ("Where a gap starts", below); the capture floor, M and the start bound (CH-22, the rule on the two clocks) are readings of the sensor's clock, and this rule never moves them back or caps them (SL-16 and SL-17 at #641's `0ed3138c`; D6's N4 on #641 at `94834c5a`). The node saves the cursor and the run file at once, writes `time.node-stepped` once for that step, and marks `clock_stepped` on any gap open when the step is found, the gap being opened or closed included, and on any gap closed and not yet written: the gap held for the floor, and a gap waiting for its budget or the ceiling (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). Then any of W, R and the floor still later than the wall clock is a step back the check could not measure: it takes the wall-clock time, and is marked and written in the same way. Only then is the time taken. A gap's written `from_utc` is never moved. **A gap marked `clock_stepped` is never closed as covered by the chain:** it closes unwatched (`covered: false`), at the first current heartbeat after its cause ends that shows capture (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`). **At a start** the check cannot measure a step made during the stop, so it can only cap, **and the cap waits for the `node-start` gap's close.** While that gap is open, nothing places W or R and no other gap opens. A cap saved then would move W past the stop that the gap holds, and a kill before the gap is written would lose the stop. The check at its close caps W, R and the floor before its `to_utc` is taken, and a check at a tick before then only measures. **The comparison at the start marks the `node-start` gap `clock_stepped` at once** where W, R or the floor is later than the wall clock; the mark moves nothing, so the gap's `open` record names the step, and only the cap waits (D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). W and R are capped as the floor is, because a gap starts at the earlier of them and the floor's raise lifts each only where it is earlier. Capping R alone would be enough, since R has a value whenever W has one; the check treats every value it moves alike.

  **A crash between an unwatched close and the save of its raise** must leave neither the gap lost nor W and R below its end. The closing event is in another database, so the raise is saved first, with the closed gap held in the cursor as the gap held for the floor (CH-18), and the event commits after it; the first cursor save after the commit clears it. At a start, a gap found there is written first, `closed` with `covered: false` and its own times and marks. One gap is held there at a time, from the raise until its event commits: a gap other than `lost` that closes unwatched before then is merged into it, its causes added, its `to_utc` taken, its number of gaps raised by one, `clock_stepped` carried if either part carries it and `from_bound` kept as the held gap's, and the raise to that end saved with it; one event is written for the whole, and while it waits no record of a later gap of the same sensor other than `lost` is written ("Budgets and merging", below). The run file's copy is the earliest of W, R and the held gap's `from_utc` as the node holds them, the gap held from the raise itself, and while a gap is held the copy never rises: each write takes the earlier of the new value and the copy it replaces, since a step back moves W, R and the copy but not the held gap's `from_utc`. #644 §1.9 item 5 states the mechanism.

  **What it costs:** a gap after a flat stretch shorter than `blind_after_s` writes up to that stretch of watched time inside the gap, which fails closed, and so does the watched interval between two gaps merged in the field. A gap closed as covered is unchanged in substance, since the chain's part 2 fails wherever a flat stretch reaches `blind_after_s` and the floor keeps every gap closed unwatched out of [R, W], and its part 4 counts the length from W, never from R (CH-39). The 60 s after which a `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap is written `open` are counted from W, and never run past 60 s after the gap's open on the monotonic clock ("When it is written", below).

  *Test:* V-10's second case: no heartbeat after the last current one, ticks continuing, and the connection reset 20 s after that heartbeat's receipt. `from_utc` is no later than that receipt, cause `link`. *Control:* a node with the ninth cause intact that opens `link` at the close starts it 20 s late. V-11 to V-13 each gain the same case, with the fork's second connection, the applied revocation or the panic 20 s after that receipt. *Control:* a gap opened at its decision starts 20 s late. V-10's third case, V-16's fourth case and V-17's fifth to eighth cases test R; V-16's fifth case and V-17's ninth case test the bound on W and R; V-9's third and fifth to eleventh cases, V-10's fourth case, V-14's second to seventh cases, V-15's second case and V-16's sixth to eighth cases test the floor, the step rule and the field.
- **Within a run, a gap's length is taken on the node's monotonic clock, and its written times on the wall clock** (D3's item 5 for #645; SL-16; D6's N1 on #641). A step forward while a gap is open does not change the count the chain's fourth part asks for (CH-39). A step back is found by the step check and marks the gap `clock_stepped`, and **a gap so marked is never closed as covered by the chain:** it closes unwatched (D1's ruling on #641 at `bb7f0d91`). A step back that puts the written end before the start is handled as across a restart (below): the gap is written with `clock_stepped: true`, and **a gap is never dropped for its times.** *Test:* V-9's third case. *Controls:* a node that lets the chain close a stepped gap as covered; and a node that takes the length from the wall-clock times, which leaves the cut with a thirty-minute step forward open.
- **The gap at the node's start reaches back** (D4-90): to the earlier of W and R, the last moment the node holds durably as watched and the last rise saved with it. **When the cursor holds no W,** whatever the reason (the cursor unread, the sensor database lost, or a sensor never watched since its enrolment), it reaches back to **an earlier moment the node still holds durably, never a later one** (SL-16; gap leg 9): the copy of the earlier of W and R in `sensorrun.json` when the file holds one, whatever its `write_no`, since a file that is behind only moves the start earlier; and otherwise the time of the live enrolment's `sensor.enrolled` record (`enrolled_at` in `sensors.json`, which the fold can also read from the chain). **A sensor never watched since its enrolment has been unwatched since it,** so its gap, opened at a node start or while the node runs, begins no later than `enrolled_at` (SL-16), and it stands from the enrolment, not from a connection's end: the `link` row above holds it while the node holds no W, so through a long first backlog, or a first dial that never comes, it is `open` in the stream (gap leg 15). The event then carries `from_bound: true`, and status says that the start is a bound and not the moment watching stopped. So a station power loss that also takes the sensor database leaves no interval in no gap.
- **Across a node restart the reach-back compares wall-clock times,** the saved moment and the time after the start, because only wall-clock times survive a reboot, and across a restart of the process alone the node keeps no other (as for C8-5's cap; not using the host's boot-relative clock there is D3's choice, on cost: SL-16; D6's N4 on #641 at `6723bf90`). A clock stepped forward during the outage lengthens the gap, which fails closed. **A clock stepped back** can put the gap's end before its start: the gap is still written from the saved moment, never dropped, with `clock_stepped: true`, and the node writes C4's `time.node-stepped` health event once for that step. The comparison at the start marks the `node-start` gap `clock_stepped` at once where W, R or the floor is later than the wall clock, and the step check at that gap's close caps them at the wall clock (the floor, above). What no check finds is stated in CH-39's part 4. C4 0.3 defines `time.node-stepped` (`contracts/event/event.go`), and nothing on `main` writes it, so this adds a writer and changes no contract (#644 §1.9 item 5; SL-16).
- **How a gap closes.**
  - **As covered,** whole, only by the chain of CH-39, in the run of W's heartbeat, with its four parts and `HeartbeatGapMax`. There is no partial cover (CH-39).
  - **Otherwise unwatched,** at the first current heartbeat after its cause ends **that shows capture** (as for W above: not every interface blind under CH-31, flat counts included), written as unwatched (`covered: false`) from its start to that heartbeat's receipt (D3's item 4 for #645; SL-16). A sensor whose counts are still flat at the reconnect keeps the gap open until an interface's `packets` rise. *Test:* V-17.
  - **Three causes are not closed by a chain** (SL-16): a **`lost`** gap is its span, from the stated `from_utc` to the fixed cut end (CH-40), written once with those times; a **`blind`** gap closes unwatched at the first current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose (CH-31; V-17's second case); a **`revoked`** gap is closed only by the first current heartbeat, showing capture, of a run under a later enrolment's generation, which is a new key, since a revoked pin is never enrolled again. It closes unwatched, never as covered: no chain runs across a new generation's run 1, and nothing the revoked key sent is trusted for that interval. Nothing else closes it: not a restart, a change of configuration or time. With no later enrolment it stays open (D3's rule 1 on #641, comment 6838033945).
  - A `held` gap after a new key's enrolment closes the same way, unwatched at that key's first current heartbeat, because no chain runs across a generation (gap leg 11).
- **What is written:** the health event `sensor.coverage-gap` on `platform/health`, with the details `cause` (the cause at the open), `causes` (every cause seen while it stood, space-separated), `state` (`open` or `closed`), `from_utc`, and when closed `to_utc` and `covered` (`true` or `false`); the times are on the node's clock, except a `lost` gap's, which are its span's. Also `merged`, the number of gaps a merged record carries; `from_bound: true` when `from_utc` is an earlier bound and not W; and `clock_stepped: true` when the node's clock was stepped back across the gap. **The names `from_bound`, `clock_stepped` and `enrolled_at` are D5's** (#644 §1.9 item 5 and §1.3; SL-16 states only what each must carry), and C13 states them so that both documents give one name. They are details of a health event, which C4 0.3 accepts as free keys; C4 0.4 does not change that.
- **When it is written** (#644 §1.9 item 5): a `lost` gap is written once, `closed` with `covered: false` and its span's times, from the save that admits its span (CH-40; SL-16: "written once with those times"); a `blind`, `held`, `revoked` or `sensor-failed` gap is written `open` when it opens; a `link`, `node-start` or `silent` gap is written `open` only once it has stood 60 s from W (from `enrolled_at` for a sensor never watched), or 60 s after it opened on the node's monotonic clock where that is earlier, since across a restart, or from `enrolled_at`, no step moves the wall-clock time it counts from (SL-16; D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`), as `sensor-unreachable` is due. Every gap written `open` is written again when it closes, covered or not. One closed before it was written `open` is written once: as `closed` with `covered: false` if unwatched, and not at all if covered. So a link drop whose backlog covers it within a minute writes nothing, a longer one is forwarded as open and then as covered, and no gap that closes as covered is forwarded as unwatched. Watched time can still lie inside a gap closed unwatched: the stretch from R to W that its start takes in, whatever a failed chain leaves whole, since there is no partial cover, and the interval between two gaps merged in the gap held for the floor. All fail closed. **A gap that never closes is in the stream as `open`** (gap legs 10 to 13), so an outage that does not end is never a quiet stream.
- **Budgets and merging.** `lost` gaps have their own 4 an hour, and every other cause another 4 (the budget table above). Past a budget, gaps of that budget are merged into one record: first start, last end, number and causes, and `clock_stepped` if any of them carries it (D6's N1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). **A `lost` gap merges only with `lost` gaps,** so it is never merged away without its cause, whatever the other budget does. A gap waiting for its budget or the ceiling is held in the cursor, as above.
- **The order of a sensor's gap records, and how a reader pairs them** (SL-16 at #641's `0ed3138c`; D6's B3 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`). While the closing record of a gap other than `lost` waits, held for the floor (CH-18) or waiting for its budget or the ceiling, the node writes no record of a later gap of the same sensor other than `lost` (D6's B2 on #641 at `50a67730`), so a gap merged into a waiting record was never written `open`; a waiting `lost` record holds nothing back, and one sensor's waiting record holds back no other sensor's. A `closed` record pairs with every `open` record of the same sensor, written before it and not yet paired, whose `from_utc` equals its own; nothing pairs by interval. While the node runs there is at most one such record; across a node stop there can be two, a gap written `open` and still open at the stop and the `node-start` gap from the same earlier of W and R, and the `node-start` gap's close ends both (D6's N4 on #641 at `50a67730`). An `open` record that no record pairs reads as a gap still open, so nothing reads as closed that its own record did not close. The 60 s by which a gap is written `open` yields to this order as it yields to the ceiling and the budget. *Test:* V-6's second and third cases, and V-14's sixth and eighth cases.
- **At a start,** the node first writes any gap held for the floor (CH-18), then opens `node-start` from the earlier of W and R for each configured sensor (the bound when it holds no W), marked `clock_stepped` at once where W, R or the floor is later than the wall clock; the step check at that gap's close caps them at the wall clock (the floor, above), and writes the `lost` gap of every span still marked owed (CH-40).
- **What it rests on.** A heartbeat is the sensor's word (AP-36). These rules stop an honest fault from closing a gap, not a compromised sensor from claiming capture it did not make.

- **Meets:** SL-16, SL-20; C8-7.
- **Test:** the rig's SL-T13 legs, named in #644 §1.9 item 8; K-5 and K-6 for the pool's per-name cap, `sensor.counters`' spacing and what waits; the gap legs V-1 to V-17 (C8-7's seventeen); H-10 for the held events after a run file lost or behind.

**Still for D5 to build, beside the table:**
- CH-14's change of order for `sensor-id`;
- CH-37's close, for every enrolment;
- CH-39's backlog by `current`, its `made_utc` window, and its marks on `collection.run`;
- CH-41's `skewed`, for every cause, with the status counts;
- CH-40's span checks against Ds, its spans and owed marks in the cursor, `spans_cut`, one cut end for each span, its `lost` gap and `node.lost`;
- CH-16's `prior` in the cursor, with its owed mark, and `sensor.run-loss` with the run's identity;
- CH-18's cursor and `MaxLinkCursorBytes`, `write_no`, and D1's alarm half;
- CH-26's and CH-27's heartbeat baselines;
- CH-12's own count of a replaced host's tail, from the `newest_seq` in the cursor;
- the coverage gaps above, with W;
- `sensor-buffer-small`;
- CH-13 row 5b's save of the new generation on a resume under a higher generation, and the generation at which the position began;
- the dial limit of CH-1 and SL-30, with `sensor-dial-limit`, the lost database and "spent" until a re-arm, and the `sensor-egress-absent` alarm;
- `closed-before-hello` (CH-11);
- the bridge build's node items (CH-35). **For the third part's brief:** the dialer's deny corpus (ruling 8).

## 14. Clauses the engine's result could change [S2]

E-D4-03 (P4-4-S2) has run: the counted run from `main` (run 43092601088, `CI-Linux`, on a hosted runner; D4-88), with the pipe case of #630, and D1 approved the engine as Zeek 9.0.1 with the project's script (D4-90). **The markers stay** (D5's N11 on #634): the pipe case read finished captures from a pipe, not a live stream, so it shows no back-pressure, no stall and no endless stream, and the third part's rig settles each clause below. These are every clause that carries the marker, and no other:

1. **CH-12:** how the agent sees the engine's output end, and so learns of a restart.
2. **CH-21:** `made_utc` − `last_utc` is the engine's delay before it writes a line. **Assumption A6:** Zeek writes a connection's record when the connection ends unless a script writes one at its start. Whether an engine can give a flow's `start` at its first frame decides whether the flow order stated below holds (difference 2). This changes the value R6.4 will report, not its measure (D6).
3. **CH-26:** `drops_decoder` assumes that the engine reads the frame stage's pipe under SL-3. E-D4-03 as built reads capture files (D7 on #625), so the marker waits for its pipe case. CH-26 also marks which program keeps `decoder_errors`, `table_evictions`, the `dnp3_*` counters and `sessions_unparsed`.
4. **CH-26, and 0.1 §4.3:** whether an engine that reads a stream keeps its own timers through a quiet spell, so that `flow_idle_s`, `sa_reply_timeout_ms` and a flow's `end: idle` do not wait for the next frame. An inference, not measured; the pipe case decides it, or the agent applies those bounds on its own clock.
5. **CH-32:** the manifest's `program` and `file` lines for the engine.
6. **CH-34:** what a line of the engine's output is.
7. **CH-39:** a flow that began in a break, whose first record the engine writes only at its end, is not a backlog record (D3).
8. **CH-42:** how the engine's clean stop leaves a marker (A8).

**Not a clause, but the engine decides it too** (D2's note on round two). Where two PRP LANs tag differently, one flow's frames carry two VIDs (CH-28). An engine that keys its connections on the VLAN would split such a flow into two half-sessions, and reassembly and DNP3 decoding would then fail on both. That costs detections, not only counts.
- Inference (D2): Suricata's `vlan.use-for-tracking` is on by default, and whether Zeek's connection key includes the VLAN depends on its configuration.
- E-D4-03 states the setting it ran with. For RCT-carrying frames the engine should not track by VLAN.

**The flow order (difference 2, ruling).** The agent numbers records in the order it makes them, and a flow's `start` is made at the flow's first frame (0.1 §4.3, §14 item 10). So the `start` of a session's own flow precedes any flow that starts after the session's window. **[S2]** If E-D4-03 shows that the engine writes a flow only at its end, the detector (D4's code) judges a session only once its capture clock passes the session's end plus `tie_grace_s` plus `flow_idle_s`, so that an idle-ended own flow is read first. That delays `own-session-unseen` alone.

## 15. Tests

Each row can be written from the text. "Contract" means a test in `contracts/netobs` of the pure checks the third part codes there: decoding each message, judging a hello, checking an `ack`, a `tick` and a record's place in its run. "Rig" means P4-4-R1's rig, over the real link. "Control" is what must make the row fail.

| Row | Clause | Shows | Control | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M-1 | CH-5, CH-11 | Each direction's closed set: any other type from the node closes the agent's connection (`type`); an unknown type from the sensor is refused (`type`), the connection stays open; an alert-shaped record is refused | A type added to either set | Contract; rig (SL-T12) |
| M-2 | CH-2 | A node frame of 1 KiB + 1 closes the agent's connection (`frame-too-large`); a sensor frame above 64 KiB closes the node's | The cap raised | Contract |
| M-3 | CH-5, CH-6, CH-7, CH-10 | Strict decode of each hello; a missing, unknown or duplicate key, a 0.1 schema, a "decimal from 0" with a leading zero, or a value above 2^64 − 1 each fail with their reason; a heartbeat first closes (`hello`); a second hello closes | A lenient decoder | Contract |
| M-4 | CH-4 | No hello in 10 s closes at each end (`hello-timeout`, `node-hello-timeout`); 30 s with no frame closes (`idle`); acks every 10 s keep a quiet link open; a stalled handshake counts `handshake-timeout`; the node does not dial with its position unreadable | Timeouts removed | Rig |
| M-5 | CH-3, CH-43 | A second handshake from the node's key: the agent closes the first (`superseded`, in `agent.closes` of the next heartbeat); a second concurrent connection at the node closes (`second-connection`) | Both connections kept | Rig |
| M-6 | CH-1 | TLS 1.2, no client certificate, no ALPN, ALPN `otsec.netobs/0.2`: each fails as `tls`; another key: `peer-key` and C8 | ALPN not required | Contract (dialer); rig (SL-T3, SL-T8) |
| M-7 | CH-38 | A source that closes cleanly after every hello: dials no faster than one per 10 s; a source that closes with records untaken: back-off | Immediate redial on any clean close | Rig |
| M-8 | CH-11 | The node's and the agent's close sets pinned; each reason reachable | A reason added or removed | Contract |
| M-9 | CH-37 | A revocation, the revocation fast path and a replacing enrolment, each during an open connection: no record taken after the decision, no `ack`, a TLS close (`enrolment`) before held records are committed, and every one at or below `through_seq`. **A `renew` with the pin unchanged during an open connection: the connection closes (`enrolment`), the node's redials end at the agent's `binding`, each counted `closed-before-hello`, until the sensor's end holds the renewal, the pair keeps its proof, and a `held` gap stands for the interval** (D4's B1 on #633). A node restarted with an edited entry: the old connection closes as `shutdown`, and the restarted node does not dial | Records taken after the decision; the close after the commits; for the `renew` leg, a node that closes only when the pin changes (the connection stays open) | Contract (node); rig (SL-T10's close leg); node test `TestSensorEnrolClosesOldConnection` (#644 §1.3) |
| M-10 | CH-11 | A record in the node's sensor-state fold made unparseable while two sensor connections are open: both close as `state-unread`, `sensor-state-unread` stands with cause `fold-record`, a `held` gap opens for each sensor, and no SYN leaves for any sensor until the state is read. Once it is read, the node dials again, and each agent resumes from `taken` with its buffer intact. **The cost when it is never read** (D5's N10 on #634): only a `rekey` or a `replace` is then accepted, and the first hello under it is a new run whose `prior` declares the discarded buffer | A node that keeps its open connections, or dials, while its state is unread | Node test; rig |
| M-11 | CH-11, CH-38 | The agent closes after the handshake and before its `sensor-hello` (its `binding` after a `renew`): the node counts `closed-before-hello`, not `hello-timeout` and never `run-complete`, and redials under the back-off, never at once (D4's note 5 on #633; #644 §1.1) | A node that counts the close as `run-complete` and redials at once, which turns a sensor that has not taken the renewal into a dial every `RedialFloor` | Node test; rig |
| H-1 | CH-7, CH-13 | Every row of CH-13 in order, with the first failure deciding: `hello`, `binding`, `run-regressed`, `run-forked`, resume (with a falling `drops_channel`), row 5a, new run, `position-not-saved`, `pinned_by`, digest, `sensor-buffer-small`. **Each close that C8-4 audits asserts its `sensor.link_refused` record with its reason:** `binding` for a hello's `sensor_id` or generation (row 2), and `run-regressed` for a resume below what the node took (row 5a) (C8-4) | A row removed; for the audit legs, a node that closes and writes no record | Contract (SL-T9 (b), (e)) |
| H-2 | CH-13 | `runs_skipped` that disagrees raises `sensor-gap-undeclared` (`runs`) and the hello is accepted | Close on disagreement | Contract |
| H-3 | CH-12, CH-18 | The agent killed after its hello: a new run, no fork (SL-T9 (j)); the engine restarted alone, the frame stage alone, or the socket holder alone: the counter rises, the last because each stage ends when its input ends (SL-T9 (k); SL-T22); after a node restart, H-1's refusals still hold (SL-T9 (g)) | The counter synced after the hello | Rig |
| H-4 | CH-6, CH-13, CH-18, CH-43 | A `taken.seq` above the agent's newest: not applied, `taken_ahead` counted, true hello sent; the node closes it as `run-regressed` (row 5a) and writes `sensor.link_refused` (`run-regressed`) (C8-4); a fork-held sensor is still not dialled after a node restart | `taken` applied blindly; for the audit leg, no record | Contract; rig |
| H-5 | CH-12 | A `renew` with the same pin after an expiry: the outage's records arrive under the new generation, none discarded. A `renew` that names a different pin: the position does not carry over | Carry-over decided by the reason alone | Rig |

(d) and (e): a node with the first half alone, which ties each. (f): a node that refuses by numbering, no record numbered at or before the `newest_seq` of the tick that shows the move, which ties it. (g): a node that takes K's baseline afresh at each hello, which ties it. (h): a node without the closure for the node's side, which ties it. (i): a node that takes a K read after a held loop as the baseline, which ties it. (j): a node that sets the floor to each raise's value, which ties it. (k): a node with no M, the floor held in memory only, which ties it; and a node that applies the step rule to the capture floor, M and the start bound, capping each at the node's wall clock at the `node-start` gap's close, which ties it. (l): a node that starts with no floor, no M and no raise from the first break's heartbeats, which ties it; and a node that applies the step rule to the capture floor, M and the start bound, capping each at the node's wall clock at the `node-start` gap's close, which ties it. (m): a node with M but no raise from the first break's heartbeats, which ties it. (n): a node that raises the floor only from the heartbeats of the first hello's run, which ties it. (o): a node that raises the floor from the heartbeats but not from stated spans, which ties it. (p): a node that raises the floor only from spans stated up to the first current hello, which ties it. (q): a node that reads M as none with the lower generation's position, which ties it. The counts: a node that counts every refusal as a clock move, and a node that shows no floor | Node tests `TestTieStoredTimeAfterNodeStep`, `TestTieRecordBeforeSensorStep` and `TestTieNodeMovedSinceSample` (#644 §1.5) |

The result decides whether CH-27's `interface_check` must require offload off, or the buffer must hold the largest coalesced frame | A holder that blocks on a full pipe (its timestamps then lag the replay); a holder that cuts long frames silently; for the alarm, a node that raises nothing on a rise of `frames_outgoing`, or lifts the alarm by itself; for the bring-up leg, a node that raises only on a rise within a run (it misses the frame) | Rig, with SL-T22 |
