**The coverage-gap legs** (CH-45; C8-7's seventeen legs in #641, leg 14 being #644's `TestGapHeldInW`; legs 16 and 17, and the second and third cases of legs 1, 9 and 10, are D6's B1 to B3 and N1 on #641 as D3 took them at `467c1dd6`; every further case follows the gap leg of the same number in D3's note at #641's `e164e416`, unchanged at `71fde2ac`, in the note's words or as D3's list "For #645" at `71fde2ac` quotes them). Test names are #644's (§1.9 item 5). Node tests on the test clock in `platform/node/sensortest`, named as #644 §1.9 item 5 names them. Each control is the change that must make its leg fail. Unless a leg says otherwise, the connection is reset at the cut, with no heartbeat withheld before it, and every dial is refused until the cut ends.

| Row | Leg | Shows | Control | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| V-1 | 1 | The node and the sensor stopped together for ten minutes, then started: one gap reaches from no later than the last heartbeat the node read while the link was current to the first that shows capture after the start, and no moment of the outage is outside a gap. **Second case** (D6's B3 on #641 at `9d74bb73`): the same with the test source's clock five minutes ahead of the node's: `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt of the last current heartbeat | A gap that begins at the node's start, which leaves the outage in none; for the second case, a node that keeps the heartbeat's `at_utc` as W, which starts the gap five minutes late | `TestGapNodeAndSensorStopped`, with the second case as its leg |
| V-2 | 2 | The link cut for ten minutes while the sensor captures, in one run, a heartbeat every interval: the gap opened at the drop closes as covered once the backlog is through, and nothing is forwarded as unwatched | A gap closed at the reconnect, before the backlog is through | `TestGapLinkCutCovered` |
| V-3 | 3 | The same with **no observations, while every interface's `packets` keeps rising** (frames the engine names nothing for): closed as covered on the heartbeats. Until `467c1dd6` this leg said "no traffic on the monitored link", which under CH-31's rule is a blind stretch and must not close as covered; V-17 tests that | A gap closed only by observations, which stays open | `TestGapQuietLinkCovered` |
| V-4 | 4 | The link cut for ten minutes with the sensor host stopped for five of them: the whole ten minutes stay open, cause `restart` | The whole break closed at the reconnect | `TestGapHostStoppedStaysOpen` |
| V-5 | 5 | A ten-minute break, longer than `backlog_tie_s`, with a sensor restart inside it and heartbeats whose stated times fill it: the whole break stays open, cause `restart` | A gap closed on the heartbeats' stated times | `TestGapRestartInLongBreak` |
| V-6 | 6 | A connection killed every 5 s for an hour, the test source beginning a new run at each kill, with one declared loss among the drops: the `lost` gap is written with its cause **Second case, a merge under a budget:** the budget of the causes other than `lost` spent within the hour but for one record; a `link` gap that stands two minutes is written `open` with that record, the node's wall clock is stepped back five minutes while it is open, and it closes unwatched past the budget; two more gaps follow, each closing unwatched within 30 s with a sensor restart inside it, and are merged with it: once the budget allows, one record holds the three, from the first gap's start, `merged` 3, `covered: false`, `clock_stepped: true`, and it pairs with the first gap's `open` record, so no `open` record of the leg is left unpaired. **Third case, a waiting `lost` record and another sensor's waiting record:** the `lost` budget spent within the hour, so a fifth `lost` record waits for it; then a `link` gap of the same sensor stands 90 s: its `open` record is in the health stream within 60 s plus one node tick of its open. The same with a second sensor, whose budget of the causes other than `lost` is spent and whose closing record waits, and a `link` gap of the first sensor that stands 90 s: its `open` record is in the stream within the same bound (D6's B2 on #641 at `50a67730`) | One budget for every cause, which the other gaps spend, so the `lost` gap is merged away; for the second case, a node that writes a merge under a budget from the last gap's start, which leaves the first gap's `open` record unpaired; and a node that keeps the last gap's mark only, which writes no `clock_stepped`; for the third case, a node that holds every later gap behind any waiting record of the same sensor, which writes the first `open` record only when the `lost` budget returns; and a node that holds a sensor's later gaps behind another sensor's waiting record, which writes the second only when that sensor's budget returns | `TestGapLostOwnBudget`; and `TestLostGapNotBehindOtherGaps` |
| V-7 | 7 | A ten-minute break in one run whose `seq`s are unbroken, with no heartbeat for eight minutes of it: open. The same with a 40 s silence just before the reconnect, between the last buffered heartbeat and the first live one: open | A chain judged by unbroken `seq`s alone (closes the first); a spacing check that leaves out the chain's live end (closes the second) | `TestGapSilentEngineStaysOpen` |
| V-8 | 8 | A ten-minute gap with the sensor's clock at about half speed: 15 heartbeats, each 20 s apart by `at_utc`: open, since 20 are needed at 30 s | A chain checked by spacing alone, which closes it | `TestGapChainCount` |
| V-9 | 9 | The sensor database deleted while the node and the sensor are stopped for ten minutes: the start gap reaches back to no later than the run file's copy of the earlier of W and R, and with that file also deleted to `enrolled_at`, with `from_bound: true`; in the second case no `taken` is sent and no source port leaves until a re-arm. A clock stepped back across the outage: the gap is written from the saved moment with `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written once. **Third case, with the node running:** a ten-minute link cut in one run with a full chain, the wall clock stepped back fifteen minutes five minutes into it: the gap closes with `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true`, and `time.node-stepped` is written once; and the same cut with a full chain and the wall clock stepped forward thirty minutes five minutes into it: the gap closes as covered. **Fourth case, a step back while the node runs:** heartbeats current, the node's wall clock stepped back five minutes, then two minutes later the node and the sensor stopped together and the sensor database deleted before the start; the test makes the node write `sensorrun.json` while it holds W, one minute before the step, and nothing else writes that file between that write and the stop: `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt, on the new clock, of the last current heartbeat, and `time.node-stepped` is written once. **Fifth case, the floor and a step back:** a `silent` gap closes unwatched one minute before the node's wall clock is stepped back five minutes, heartbeats current after it, and two minutes after the step the node and the sensor stop together and start again, the sensor database kept: the `node-start` gap's `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt, on the new clock, of the last current heartbeat. **Sixth case, the floor after a step during a stop:** heartbeats current every 10 s with `packets` rising; the node alone stopped for one minute, the sensor capturing and its database kept, and the node's clock stepped back five minutes during the stop; the `node-start` gap, marked `clock_stepped`, closes with `covered: false` once the backlog is through, and the connection is reset 30 s after that close: the `node-start` gap's record carries `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true`, and the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no later than the node's receipt of the last current heartbeat before the reset. **Seventh case, W and R capped:** heartbeats current, then the node and the sensor stop together for one minute, the sensor database kept, and the node's clock is stepped back five minutes during the stop; the connection is reset 2 s after the node's receipt of the heartbeat that closes the `node-start` gap, unwatched, before the next heartbeat: the `link` gap's `from_utc` is no later than that receipt. **Eighth case, a step between two ticks:** heartbeats current every 10 s with `packets` rising, the node's wall clock stepped back five minutes 3 s after a heartbeat's receipt, and the connection reset 5 s after the step, the test clock running no node tick and the source sending no heartbeat between the step and the reset: `from_utc` is no later than that heartbeat's receipt on the new clock, and `time.node-stepped` is written once. **Ninth case, E's samples:** ticks on time every 10 s; the test source raises the delay of its heartbeats by 10 s a heartbeat to 120 s and holds it there, `packets` rising, as in V-16's fifth case; then it stops making heartbeats and keeps sending those it holds; the node's wall clock is stepped back 60 s, 1 s after a tick's receipt and 2 s before the receipt of the last heartbeat the source holds, with no tick between: the `silent` gap's `from_utc` is no later than that heartbeat's `at_utc` + E on the new clock. **Tenth case, the cap waits for the `node-start` gap's close:** heartbeats current; the node and the sensor stop together for one minute, the sensor database kept, and the node's clock is stepped back five minutes during the stop; the node starts with the sensor still stopped, the test clock runs a node tick and a cursor save 10 s after the start, and the node is killed 20 s after the start, before the `node-start` gap is written `open`, then started again with the sensor running: the `node-start` gap after the second start has its `from_utc` at the moment saved before the first stop, with `clock_stepped: true`. **Eleventh case, the step check's threshold:** a ten-minute link cut in one run with a full chain, the node's wall clock running slow against its monotonic clock by 0.5 ms each second throughout, as a slew does, 300 ms over the cut: the gap closes as covered, with no `clock_stepped` and no `time.node-stepped`, at any interval between the node's checks, since the wall clock never runs backward and no two checks see it fall more than 300 ms behind; and the same cut with one step back of 3 s five minutes into it and no slew: the gap closes with `covered: false` and `clock_stepped: true` | A gap that begins at the node's start, with its start marked unknown (the ten minutes in none); a gap dropped when its end is before its start; for the third case, a node that lets the chain close a stepped gap as covered, which writes `covered: true`; and a node that takes the gap's length from its wall-clock times, which asks the chain for 80 heartbeats, holds about 60, and leaves the gap open; for the fourth case, a node that keeps W and its copy on the old clock, which starts the gap at that copy, about two minutes after that receipt (less up to one heartbeat interval); for the fifth case, a node that moves W, R and the copy back with the step but not the floor, which starts the gap about two minutes after that receipt; for the sixth case, a node that lets the chain close the stepped `node-start` gap as covered, which writes `covered: true`; and a node that caps the floor at no point and keeps it, at an unwatched close, as the later of its value and that close's `to_utc`, which holds W and R at the saved moment and starts the `link` gap there, about three and a half minutes after that receipt; for the seventh case, a node that caps the floor but caps W and R at no point, which the close does not raise because they are later than its end, and which starts the gap at the saved moment, about four minutes after that receipt (a node that caps R alone passes, as SL-16 allows); for the eighth case, a node that runs the step check only at its tick, which starts the gap at W on the old clock, about five minutes late; and a node that moves W and R to the wall-clock time at the gap's open, and not back by the step, which starts it 8 s after that receipt; for the ninth case, a node that moves W, R, the floor and the copy back by the step but not E's samples, which places W and R at that heartbeat's `at_utc` + E on the old clock and starts the gap about 60 s late; for the tenth case, a node that caps W, R and the floor at the start, or at its first tick, and saves them, which starts that gap at the first start's wall-clock time, with no `clock_stepped`, and leaves the first stop in no gap; for the eleventh case, a node that acts on any backward difference, which marks the gap at its first check in the cut and closes it unwatched; and a node that acts only on a step of a minute or more, which closes it as covered | `TestGapLostDatabaseReachesBack`, with the third case as its leg |
| V-10 | 10 | Never back: the link cut and never restored. Within 60 s plus one node tick a `link` gap is in the health stream as `open`, and it stays open. **Second case** (D6's B3 on #641 at `467c1dd6`, which replaces the case of `9d74bb73`): the test source sends no heartbeat after the last current one, keeps sending ticks, and resets the connection 20 s after that heartbeat's receipt, before the ninth cause falls due. `from_utc` is no later than that receipt, and the cause is `link`. **Third case, a gap that never closes after a flat stretch** (D6's B1 on #641 at `da7a84ee`): every interface's `packets` flat, carrier `up`, from four minutes before W, then the connection reset at W and never restored. a node that closes this cause only as unwatched, which writes `covered: false` in the second; for the third case, a node that ends this cause at the first current heartbeat judges a chain of two where the fourth part asks for four, and writes `covered: false`; for the fourth case, a gap that keeps W as its start begins four minutes late; for the fifth to seventh cases, the controls each states; for the eighth case, a node that runs the step check only at its tick, at placements and at opens, which writes the `silent` gap without `clock_stepped` and starts the `link` gap about 15 s before t1 | `TestGapHeartbeatSilence` |

A `blind` gap is written `open` from no later than the node's receipt of the last heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose (R, placed as W is), and closes with `covered: false` at the first current heartbeat in which one rises **Cases three to seven** (D6's B1 and B2 on #641 at `467c1dd6`). The third to sixth are each a ten-minute link cut from W, in one run, with a heartbeat every 10 s and the backlog through, and each gap closes with `covered: false`. **Third: every carrier `down` for 60 s inside the cut,** `packets` rising before and after. **Fourth: every interface's `packets` flat for seven minutes inside the cut,** carrier `up`. **Fifth: every interface flat from four minutes before W, the node alone restarted inside the cut, and flat until two minutes after W.** Also, `from_utc` is no later than R. **Sixth: the same flat stretch with no restart:** `from_utc` is no later than R. **Seventh: the cables pulled 3 s after W's heartbeat,** the link current: the next heartbeat shows `packets` risen, from frames before the pull, and every carrier `down`. A `blind` gap opens with `from_utc` no later than W. **Eighth: the sixth case's flat stretch with a new run inside the cut** (D6's B1 on #641 at `da7a84ee`). The old run's heartbeats arrive as a backlog before the new run's hello, since the old run is flushed first and no record of an older run follows a newer run's hello (CH-16, SL-14), and show 420 s flat. **Clauses with nothing to test:** CH-24 (no measure changes), CH-25 (its stream is exercised by every row of §3), CH-30 (options). CH-45's caps are tested by #644's SL-T13 legs (#644 §1.9 item 8), and its gaps by V-1 to V-17.

**Changes owed to the rig** (D6's notes on #626; D6's to make after approval):

- `c13_source.py` writes the 0.2 identifier, `made_utc`, `config_digest`, `prp_window_ms`, `interfaces`, `agent` and the new counters.
- It becomes a two-way peer: a TLS 1.3 listener with a test key and ALPN; it reads `node-hello`, sends `sensor-hello` and ticks with `sent_seq`, and moves its trim cursor by `ack`. Its four captures become four connections with `run_counter` 1 to 4, `prior` and `run-complete`.
- The fault modes of rows H, S and A.
- `gt_checker.py` accepts the new fields and asserts `made_utc`.
- A 0.1 stream is kept for R-1 to R-3.

## 16. Not in 0.2, assumptions and limits

**Not in 0.2:**

- A count of frames the NIC or its driver dropped before the packet socket. `drops_driver` does not include them (CH-26). A candidate for a later minor.
- The "MAC changed within a flow" signal (D4-73's candidate list). The brief does not ask for it.
- HSR, which stays `not-decoded`.
- A merge of two PRP sensors' streams (CH-30, option (iii)).
- Any time, setting or command from the node to the sensor (SL-17, SL-19).

**Assumptions,** each `unknown` until what is named settles it:

| | Assumption | Settled by | If it is false |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | `PACKET_STATISTICS` returns `tp_packets` and `tp_drops` and resets them on read (CH-26) | Reading packet(7); W-3 | `drops_driver` double-counts or under-counts |
| A2 | An unprivileged process can read an interface's carrier (CH-31) | K-3 | `carrier` reads `unknown` |
| A3 | The link agent, with no capability, can read an interface's addresses, ARP flag, IPv6 state, master and upper devices, and `ip_forward`, through sysfs and rtnetlink (CH-27) | W-2, C-11's negative control | `interface_check` reads `unknown` |
| A4 | IEC 62439-3 gives `EntryForgetTime` as 400 ms; the RCT's sequence number is 16 bits; and the LAN ID's place in the RCT (CH-28, CH-30) | The standard's text, which the project does not hold; a site's PRP capture, with the owner's authorization | The window's basis and the wrap figure change. A4 cannot lose a frame, because only an identical copy is discarded and a full table passes frames (D3) |
| A5 | The sensor host's file system keeps what was synced across a power cut (CH-12, CH-19) | An H-row | A repeated counter under a new `run_id` reads as a fork: a held link and a site visit (SL-16; D3). A buffer that lost synced records that the node took closes as `run-regressed` at every dial (CH-13 row 5a; D3) |
| A6 | Zeek writes a connection's record at its end unless a script writes one at its start (§14) | E-D4-03 | The detector's fallback of §14 |
| A7 | A mirrored station LAN carries periodic traffic (CH-31) | D2's device sources show the devices send it; whether a mirror carries it is per site, with the owner's authorization | False `sensor-blind`; hence the 300 s default |
| A8 | The engine's service manager can record whether the engine stopped cleanly, for CH-42's marker | E-D4-03 and the sensor's image | An engine stop always reads `unclean` (fails loud) |
| A9 | Listing nftables rules needs `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, so the link agent cannot read the egress rule (CH-27) | W-6 | If false, `egress_check` is carried and the visibility limit below does not hold |
| A10 | A send with `PACKET_QDISC_BYPASS` does not reach packet taps, so `frames_outgoing` misses it (CH-26) | W-5 records it on the rig's kernel | If false, the counter sees more; it stays a live view and never a proof either way |
| A11 | A commit of the sensor database survives a power cut on the node's Windows host, where a file's rename may not (D4-65's open question; SL-30, SL-31). It is assumed of commits only: creating a database renames `<path>.new` into place, with no folder flush on Windows (`platform/store/db.go`, `os_windows.go`, `desk`) | A power-cut H-row on the node's hardware, with the owner's authorization | A committed cursor could come back older, and with it the position, the spans, `prior` and the digest, which SL-31 rests on keeping with D. The rules that read a missing database, or one with no write number, as lost still fail closed; a cursor that is merely older is not detected |

**Limits:**

- **Everything is `desk`, and nothing is built.** No row of §15 has run.
- **A compromised sensor can still lie** in every field it states: counts, `drops_channel`, `lost`, `prior`, `previous_end`, `config_digest`, `pinned_by`, `made_utc` (and so `late`), and every observation (AP-34 (b), AP-36). 0.2 makes a lie by another sensor, a replay, a copy and an undeclared loss visible. It does not make a sensor honest.
- **`late` is the sensor's own word** (CH-23). The node's own measure is CH-39's break. Its residual, a clock moved and restored within a break at or below `backlog_tie_s` and within `max_skew_ms` of the node's measure, goes to AP-35 (ruling 1, D3). So does CH-22's one-tick residual, a move of the sensor's clock shown only at the first tick after a record's read, undone or not.
- **The current run can wait behind an outage's old runs:** up to 64 runs drain at one connection every 10 s, about 11 minutes, or longer under back-off (CH-38, CH-46). `sensor-no-heartbeat` shows it (D6).
- **A node that holds its key can empty the sensor's buffer** by acknowledging. The buffer is not a second evidence store (D3's note 10). A flood while the link is cut evicts earlier records, declared (CH-20); D3 adds it to AP-36.
- **A crash** loses up to `buffer_sync_ms` of numbered records, and an engine or capture-stage crash whatever that program held. Each is declared as a possible loss (CH-42), not counted exactly.
- **Two node kills can over-count one drop.** If a release of an earlier run's held records stores nothing, it moves no T and only raises the newer run's totals (CH-17), so two kills in that window can raise a dropped total twice. That is an over-count, never a loss (D6 on `0f4f19f3`).
- **A tick's offset includes queueing** behind records on the same stream (CH-22). The least of 6 ticks reduces this, and does not remove it while a long backlog drains. CH-39 covers ties during that time.
- **A `lost` span is on the sensor's clock** (CH-40; D3's item 9 for #645, not taken in 0.2). While no tie is made for skew (CH-22's two standing causes: `sensor-clock-skew` stands, or the latest o is outside `max_skew_ms`; D5's N1 on #645), the span, its gap and the interval in which `own-session-unseen` is off can be shifted by up to the offset from the interval actually lost; the cut end keeps it from reaching past the node's receipt plus `max_skew_ms`.
- **A loss's mark** reaches the detector only on a later observation of the same run (CH-40). If none follows, a session in the span may raise `own-session-unseen`, which fails loud.
- **A PRP duplicate later than the window,** and a full table, double counts with `coverage.degraded` empty (CH-28, CH-29). The counters show the second, and nothing shows the first.
- **How long the buffer lasts** is not known, because no station's record rate has been measured (CH-20).
- **The sensor may not report whether its egress rule is loaded.** If the link agent cannot read the rule without a capability (A9), `egress_check` is left out of every heartbeat (CH-27). That is a visibility limit: the node then learns of the rule only through the digest's `file` line, which shows the file and not the kernel's rules (CH-32), and the rule is shown only by SL-T21 on the rig.
- **The only measurement behind SL-3's shape is one kernel, on a veth pair** (run 43077596916, with a stand-in and no sensor). Nothing in this proposal about the four programs, the first pipe or the egress rule is built, and no sensor image exists.
- **The gap rule reports watched time as unwatched when it cannot show otherwise** (CH-39, CH-45). A sensor restart inside any break, a declared loss, an unread `seq`, a silent stretch or too few heartbeats leaves the whole gap open. That is the direction D4-90 requires, and it costs false "unwatched" intervals, never false "watched" ones. A compromised sensor can still state heartbeats that close a gap (AP-36).
- **After a lost sensor database** (CH-18): a stale run-file copy can let one narrow window of copied or restored runs in (D3's bound); every held span is taken again as a new `lost` gap; `sensor-gap-undeclared` is raised once for the trimmed records; a `prior` owed may be lost, named in status; a capture loss can be counted twice; and the next dial waits for an administrator's re-arm.
- **A `sensor.run-loss` or a `lost` gap can be written twice** after a kill between its commit and the clearing of its mark (CH-16, CH-40). Never not at all; the run's identity in the event lets a reader see it.
- **The agent's counts are in status only** (CH-43), not in a forwarded event, because the event would not fit a 2,048-octet Syslog target with them, by an unreproduced estimate (C4 0.4, §4).

## 17. The node design's shared values, clause by clause (P4-4-L6)

#644 §1.11 lists 23 values that the node's design shares with this proposal. Brief P4-4-L6's acceptance requires that no shared rule differ in value, name or meaning. Each row below names where this proposal states it. "Same" means the value, the name and the meaning are as #644 states them. **Read for this revision against #644 at `50942c97` and D3's note at #641's `d83c07e4`** (the heads read, recorded as history). #644 at `50942c97` applies D3's list "For #644" at `d83c07e4` (D5's comment 6847960205 on #644), so row 10 now reads Same. The revision at `fa385403` read #644 at `bc19358e` and #641 at `d83c07e4`; the one at `a64fff48` read #644 at `bc19358e` and #641 at `0ed3138c`; the one before read #644 at `245e158c` and #641 at `71fde2ac`, the one before that #644 at `b4519c63` and #641 at `467c1dd6`, the second revision `4a606518` and `9d74bb73`, and the first `6061660a` and `41a073f2`. Evidence class: `desk`.

**Where the three documents still differ** (#641 at `d83c07e4`, #644 at `50942c97`, this proposal at its new head; the revisions before read #641 at `d83c07e4` and #644 at `bc19358e`, #641 at `0ed3138c` and #644 at `bc19358e`, #641 at `71fde2ac` and #644 at `245e158c`, and #641 at `467c1dd6` and #644 at `b4519c63`). Settled since the first revision: (a) a `lost` gap is written once, `closed`, in all three (#644's `TestGapLostWrittenOnce`); (b) the run's first heartbeat seen from its start sets the egress baseline and raises nothing (#644 §1.8, `TestEgressNeverCarriedNoAlarm`); (c) leg 11, the never-watched gap and the stale phrases; (d) H-8's second half in `TestCarryOverStartGeneration`; (e) D1's alarm half; (f) `capture_loss` includes `drops_driver` (D1's ruling 1 in comment 6840581561 on #645); ruling 5 on #634 itself names only `frames_overlong` beside `drops_holder`. **Settled at this revision:** (g) the `drops_driver` baseline and the cursor's 1,575 bytes, in both; (h) D1's one rule for the three capture-loss counters (6840188059), which removes the asymmetry the last revision recorded; (i) D3's items for #645 and for #644 at `467c1dd6`, applied in both, with the ninth cause's token `silent`; (j) test names for T-12, H-11, K-5, K-6, W-8 (f) and (g), V-16 and V-17, now #644's; (k) T-12 as D1 ruled it: a scheduler call's panic is taken as a timeout and raises no `sensor-failed` (#644's `TestSchedulerPanicTakenAsTimeout`). **Settled after this revision's head `1c454cb6`, in #644 at `e8ecd587`** (D5's N4 on #645; read again by D4 at #644's `e8ecd587` and `172d0cd5`): (l) #644 drops the mark "proposed" on `silent`; (m) #644 §1.11's last column says what #645 states for every row. **Settled at `df60f8fa`** (#641 at `71fde2ac`, #644 at `245e158c`): (n) `MaxLinkCursorBytes` 4,096 in all three, with R-5's case of a cursor one byte over it refused (D1's ruling, comment 6846045180 on #645); (o) the cursor's text form at most 2,026 bytes, 2,032 with `unclean` in full, and its cost 930 bytes with no span or `prior` held and up to 2,026 while they are, in both (CH-18, §11, §12, §13, §17 row 1; #644 §1.4); the 1,575 of (g) is the figure before what is held with W, the gap held for the floor and M; (p) every gap starting at the earlier of W and R, the floor and the gap held for it, the step rule, and the order and pairing of a sensor's gap records (CH-18, CH-39, CH-45; SL-16; #644 §1.9 item 5); (q) the rule on the two clocks, the capture floor carried across a node start by M and raised by the heartbeats read after the start up to the first current hello and by stated spans, and T-13 (CH-22, CH-41; SL-17; #644 §1.5). **Settled at `a64fff48`** (#641 at `0ed3138c`, #644 at `bc19358e`): (r) the agent's sync of `drops_channel` and `lost` before each tick, a message's stated spans raising the floor before its tick decides a tie (withdrawn at this head, (v)), M kept across every enrolment, the cost under sustained eviction, a sensor host that lies, the stall as an assumption, and the `silent` gap's `open` at the earlier of the two moments, in all three (CH-18, CH-19, CH-22, CH-45; SL-16, SL-17; #644 §1.4, §1.5, §1.9, §1.14); (s) the refusals by a span's raise counted apart, under #644's `skew_span_windows` (CH-41; #644 §1.5 item 5); (t) the twelfth case and the start bound in the seventh case's second control, as T-13 (p) and (k) (#644 §1.5's tests); (u) #644 §1.11's last column for row 1 now states what this proposal says at `df60f8fa` (2,026, 2,032 with `unclean` in full, 930), and its lead names #645 at `df60f8fa`. **At `fa385403`** (#641 at `d83c07e4`, #644 at `bc19358e`): (v) **no tie waits for a tick**: a tie decided at its record's read, none while the node's clock has moved more than `max_skew_ms` since the latest sample's receipt, the one-tick residual, three causes with two standing (CH-41, CH-40, §16), and T-13's (h), (q) and counts, in #641 and here (CH-22, T-13; SL-17, AP-35); **closed in #644 at `50942c97`** (§1.5, §1.11 row 10, `TestTieNodeMovedSinceSample`), so row 10 reads Same. What remains:

- **D3's item 9 (a span brought to the node's clock by E) is not taken** (CH-40, §16). D3 asked it without making it a condition. #641 and #644 state no such rule, so the documents agree; the residual is recorded here and in AP-35's sense in #641.
- Nothing else. #644 §1.11 has no "D4 changes" column at `50942c97`; its last column says "#645 states it" for rows 10, 11 and 23, as (m) records. #644 §1.8 at `50942c97` adds how an administrator clears `sensor-transmitted`, recorded by the alarm's own pool event (`TestTransmittedClear`); CH-45 cites it, and nothing in this proposal contradicts it (the alarm is in CH-45's pool and not among the alarms that write no health event).
